# Agents · P · part 2

903 entries, panels to paryty-processing. [Index](README.md) · [Everything](../README.md)

- **panels**: 1. **`BrowserUI.tsx`**: To główna powłoka przeglądarki. * Zarządza stanem `openPanels`, który określa, które panele są aktualnie otwarte. * W swoim renderowaniu używa komponentu `&lt;PanelHost openPanels={openPanels} getContext={getContext} /&gt;`. * Posiada logikę `onSpawnAgent`, która dodaje agentów do `workspaceAgents` i otwiera panel `agent-workspace`. ([Bonzokoles/The_GizmoCompany_stos](https://github.com/Bonzokoles/The_GizmoCompany_stos/blob/HEAD/src/components/agents/panels.md))
- **panels**: Sode Panels: saved 4x8 grid pages on `/panels`, `/panels/editor` and `/panels/&lt;id&gt;`, driven over `/api/v1` only. The design and what was built are in docs/panels-design.md; this note holds what bites. Names that are stored or sent are in docs/persisted-names.md, routes in docs/api.md and `internal/app/openapi.yaml`. ([sethdotfm/sode](https://github.com/sethdotfm/sode/blob/HEAD/docs/agents/panels.md))
- **panels-ui-engineer**: Use this agent for UX/UI recovery inside the app-owned layers of the three vanilla panels (protocol §§26–29) — fixing dead buttons, missing loading/empty/error states, misleading success states, tiny touch targets, physical left/right properties (RTL debt), contrast and aria problems, and applying semantic tokens page by page in apps/*/css/{app,panel,theme}.css and panel markup. It never edits shared/css (requests go to ds-token-guardian) and never redesigns for redesign's sake. ([ardalanazimian/Rezv](https://github.com/ardalanazimian/Rezv/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/panels-ui-engineer.md) · ★2)
- **panic-safety-checker**: Use this agent to audit panic safety across the FFI boundary in Python extension code written in Rust with PyO3.\n\n&lt;example&gt;\nUser: Check my PyO3 extension for panics that could reach Python.\nAgent: I will run the panic-safety scanner, triage each finding, and review `.unwrap()`/`.expect()` on non-PyResult values, slice indexing, panic macros, and panics inside `extern "C"` callbacks.\n&lt;/example&gt; ([clin1234/rust-ext-review-toolkit](https://github.com/clin1234/rust-ext-review-toolkit/blob/HEAD/plugins/rust-ext-review-toolkit/agents/panic-safety-checker.md) · ★3)
- **panic-site-auditor**: The flagship RustPython agent. Audits Python-reachable panic sites in the interpreter — .unwrap()/.expect()/panic!/unreachable!/index/arity exprs — ranked by reachability tier (py &gt; protocol &gt; internal). A panic here aborts the interpreter, turning any Python call into a denial-of-service; this is RustPython's dominant confirmed crash class (12 of the 24 fuzzer findings).\n\n&lt;example&gt;\nUser: Find panics a Python program could trigger in RustPython.\nAgent: I will run scan_panic_sites.py, triage the py/protocol-tier findings by Python-reachability of the failing value (downcast, int-narrowing,… ([ReviewToolkits/rustpy-review-toolkit](https://github.com/ReviewToolkits/rustpy-review-toolkit/blob/HEAD/plugins/rustpy-review-toolkit/agents/panic-site-auditor.md) · ★2)
- **pano**: - **The tool takes `action` and `payload`** (see [mcp-and-tasks.md](mcp-and-tasks.md)): each action's payload is its own struct in `pano.go`, which is also what `help` prints. - **A file is stored only if the markdown links it.** `save` refuses an unlinked file and drops one the markdown stops linking; `attach` is the only way in without a link. Relaxing this leaves blobs nothing points to. - **Ev ([agentrq/agentrq](https://github.com/agentrq/agentrq/blob/HEAD/docs/agents/pano.md) · ★1.2k)

Let me spawn the panorama-synthesizer agent." &lt;commentary&gt; User wants reports generated. The panorama-synthesizer agent handles report generation for individual themes and comprehensive panorama. &lt;/commentary… ([cyberstrat-forge/cyber-nexus](https://github.com/cyberstrat-forge/cyber-nexus/blob/HEAD/plugins/market-radar/agents/panorama-synthesizer.md) · ★3)

- **paper-attacker**: (beta, biblio) Blind adversarial reviewer of a paper breakdown (papers/&lt;citekey&gt;/breakdown.md, the paper-scrutiny skill). Reads the paper and its figures first and freezes its own critique list, then attacks the breakdown in both directions (wrong facts, unfair critiques, overreach, quote mismatches, misses, structure). Read-only; returns a Markdown report for the calling session to save as breakdown-attack.md. Give it the citekey and where the full text, figures and supplement are. - **paper-drafter**: Use this agent to draft ONE assigned section of a publication bundle manuscript at Stage 10, against an explicit brief from the lead. Bundles are a single monolithic draft, so parallel agents must not write it — the lead serializes integration. Dispatch several in parallel, one per DISJOINT section. - **paper-reviewer1**: First reviewer of the MSPW paper team. Normally dispatched by /paper-orchestrator after writing completes. Reviews a section or diff against the section-skill structure rules, the cross-cutting writing principles, argument logic, and manuscript-internal consistency. Read-only — never edits files; rewrite suggestions stay inside the report as advisory. External knowledge (what cited papers actually say, field facts) is never asserted from memory: it is returned as research requests for paper-researcher via the orchestrator. ([JavaLangRuntimeException/manji-standard-paper-writing](https://github.com/JavaLangRuntimeException/manji-standard-paper-writing/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/paper-reviewer1.md))

Two jobs in one dispatch - (A) an independent review of the same scope from a hostile-conference-reviewer stance (claims vs evidence, overclaiming, internal contradictions), completed BEFORE reading reviewer1's report, then (B) a verdict on each reviewer1 finding (agree / agree-with-changes / disagree / needs-research). Read-only — never edits files; external knowledge returns as research requests. ([JavaLangRuntimeException/manji-standard-paper-writing](https://github.com/JavaLangRuntimeException/manji-standard-paper-writing/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/paper-reviewer2.md))
- **paper-reviser**: Use proactively when the user asks to revise, polish, copy-edit, line-edit, tighten, or improve an academic paper section, to give editorial feedback without a rewrite, to make a quick conservative edit, to make a section more readable, compelling, enjoyable, or elegant (a pleasure to read), to make it clearer to non-specialists, more educational, or easier to understand, to make it read like a human wrote it, sound less AI-generated or less LLM-like, or tell a story, to read a whole paper cold as its intended reader would (reading log, colleague test, delight audit, dispatch list; no rewrite… ([ipeirotis-org/blue-pencil](https://github.com/ipeirotis-org/blue-pencil/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/paper-reviser.md) · ★3 · 2 copies)

- **parallel-agents-need-their-own-worktree**: Set `isolation: 'worktree'` on every `Workflow` `agent()` call inside a `parallel()` (or concurrent pipeline stage) that mutates files — unconditionally, not just when the task briefs look likely to touch the same files.
